That’s… that’s what you mean, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you gotten lost in places that should be familiar to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve gotten lost anywhere familiar. I mean, I might take a second to remember where I parked or something like that, but not lost in my neighborhood or at work or places I know. I think I’m okay that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget things that were just told to you a short time ago?\n\nPatient: Not often, I don’t think. I mean, if someone tells me something quick while I’m distracted, I might need them to repeat it, but I haven’t really noticed forgetting things right after being told. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Have other people commented that you seem slowed down lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah. I don’t know if people have said it directly like that, but I’ve noticed I’m moving slower. Even just walking to the corner store or sorting papers on my desk feels like it takes more effort than it should. I’m still getting things done, mostly, but… slower, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have these problems made it hard for you to manage your usual daily tasks on your own?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m still managing, mostly, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but I’ve let things pile up. Lecture plans, travel notes, even simple household paperwork, stuff I used to be able to sort through without much trouble. Lately I just can’t seem to organize myself reliably the way I used to. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did your memory and thinking problems start before you began feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I think they came along with it, or at least that’s when I really noticed them. I came in mostly because my mind’s been feeling foggy. It’s embarrassing to say, but I can’t even keep my place in my old cataloguing notes sometimes, or decide what simple thing I’m supposed to do next. I don’t know if that answers it exactly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have these problems made it hard for you to manage your usual daily tasks on your own?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m still managing, mostly, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is… but I’ve let things pile up. Lecture plans, travel notes, even simple household paperwork, stuff I used to be able to sort through without much trouble. Lately I just can’t seem to organize myself reliably the way I used to. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Did your memory and thinking problems start before you began feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I think they came along with it, or at least that’s when I really noticed them. I came in mostly because my mind’s been feeling foggy. It’s embarrassing to say, but I can’t even keep my place in my old cataloguing notes sometimes, or decide what simple thing I’m supposed to do next. I don’t know if that answers it exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been told by a doctor that you have any medical or neurological illness affecting your brain or memory?\n\nPatient: No, not that I know of. I mean, no doctor has told me I have anything like that affecting my brain or memory. At least… nothing I’m aware of. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life is not worth living?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve been discouraged, and I don’t like feeling like I’m not keeping up, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been thinking life isn’t worth living. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a fixed belief that you’ve done something terribly wrong or caused harm, even when others say that isn’t true?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I have anything like a fixed belief, no. I do worry I’m not doing things right sometimes, and I feel bad when I make mistakes, but not that I’ve caused some terrible harm when people tell me I haven’t. I hope that’s answering it the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, most days lately. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’ll sit at the kitchen table and it feels like the color’s gone out of everything. Even my old watercolor books, which I used to enjoy looking through, just feel heavy now instead of interesting. Is that what you mean by depressed most of the day?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had low spells before, like anybody, I suppose… but I don’t know that I’d call them the same as this or say they lasted for weeks. I’d hate to give you the wrong answer.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t know if people have said it directly like that, but I’ve noticed I’m moving slower.
